↗

Imagen ilustrativa

Decida qué cabe, quién ayuda y qué tener a mano

Tome decisiones habitación por habitación

Use las medidas de la nueva vivienda para decidir qué llevar. Empiece por los muebles de uso diario y los recuerdos personales; después revise armarios y espacios de almacenamiento. Separe las listas de venta, donación y envío para no incluir un objeto dos veces en el inventario.

Elija quién puede ayudar a coordinar

Decida quién participará en la conversación sobre el presupuesto y quién responderá preguntas en cada domicilio. Consulte cómo autorizar a un familiar u otra persona de confianza para hablar de los acuerdos. Si se muda a una comunidad administrada, pregunte por horarios de llegada, reserva de ascensor y procedimientos de recepción.

Mantenga accesibles medicamentos, documentos personales y artículos de uso diario.

Señale piezas frágiles, muy pesadas o de valor sentimental para revisarlas por separado.

Pregunte qué tareas de embalaje o manipulación pueden incluirse por escrito.

Prepare su primer día en la nueva vivienda

Separe una bolsa con medicamentos, cargadores, lentes y una muda de ropa. Comparta un plano sencillo con quien reciba el envío y marque dónde deben quedar la cama, las sillas y otros muebles de uso diario. Confirme las instrucciones de entrega con el transportista.

Imagen ilustrativa

Prepare ambas viviendas para la mudanza

Recorra toda la vivienda, también los espacios que se olvidan

Empiece en la entrada y avance habitación por habitación. Incluya los muebles detrás de una puerta cerrada, las estanterías del garaje y los objetos guardados en otro lugar. Separe lo que llevará consigo, donará o dejará. Un video breve o las medidas pueden ayudar a describir piezas grandes; pregunte qué necesita su coordinador para revisar el inventario.

Describa el recorrido desde el camión hasta cada habitación

En un apartamento, la mudanza comienza antes de llegar al ascensor. Pregunte a la administración por los horarios de entrega, la reserva del ascensor, el acceso de carga y los documentos que debe presentar el transportista. En una casa, anote entradas estrechas, escalones, ramas bajas y un lugar seguro donde hablar con el transportista sobre la descarga.

Anote el piso y los tramos de escaleras de cada domicilio.

Mida las piezas grandes y las puertas por donde deben pasar.

Designe a alguien que pueda autorizar el acceso si usted está de viaje.

Separe lo necesario para la primera noche

Reserve los artículos que necesitará antes de desempacar: documentos personales, medicamentos, cargadores y una muda de ropa. Diga al transportista qué pertenencias llevará consigo y consulte sus restricciones antes de embalar objetos poco comunes.

El transportista realiza la recogida, el transporte y la entrega. Los cargos finales dependen de la tarifa del transportista, el inventario, los servicios, la ruta y las condiciones de la mudanza. Preguntas antes de mudarse

¿Quién organiza la mudanza y quién transporta sus pertenencias?

−

Vantage describe su función como intermediario de mudanzas interestatales de bienes del hogar: organiza el transporte con una empresa transportista. El transportista asignado, y no Vantage, realiza el transporte. Identifique esa empresa en los documentos de su envío y guarde sus datos de contacto junto con el presupuesto. Consulte por separado al intermediario y al transportista en el buscador federal. Que una empresa aparezca en un directorio no significa que esté asignada a su mudanza; pida ese dato a su coordinador. Lea las condiciones por escrito antes de contratar servicios.

+

Imagen ilustrativa

Planifique el intervalo entre recogida y entrega

Explique el intervalo y lo que aún no sabe

Indique la primera fecha en que debe salir y cuándo podría recibirse el envío en destino. Aclare si esa fecha está confirmada o depende de una compraventa, contrato de alquiler u obra. Pregunte al coordinador qué acuerdos pueden estudiarse para su ruta; esta página no confirma un almacén ni una reserva de espacio.

Pregunte qué sucede entre la recogida y la entrega

Antes de aceptar el almacenamiento, pregunte quién guarda los bienes, cuánto duran las condiciones indicadas y cómo solicitar la entrega. Consulte si podrá acceder a objetos y qué aviso previo o cargos de manipulación y nueva entrega se aplicarían. Describa cualquier necesidad de temperatura, humedad o manejo especial y pida una respuesta expresa.

¿Quién presta el almacenamiento y con quién me comunico?

¿Qué cargos se aplican si el intervalo se prolonga?

¿Qué condiciones de protección y reclamación se aplican mientras los bienes están almacenados?

Compare el recorrido completo

Use la hoja de comparación para revisar juntos recogida, almacenamiento, manipulación y entrega final. Pregunte qué cambiaría si cambia la fecha o el domicilio de entrega. Guarde las instrucciones escritas para pedir la entrega junto con los datos del proveedor.

↗

Imagen ilustrativa

Decida qué embalar y dónde necesita ayuda

Separe la lista de embalaje del inventario de envío

Un mueble puede viajar sin necesitar ayuda para embalarlo, mientras que una pequeña alacena de vajilla puede requerir bastante preparación. Anote por habitación qué embalará usted y qué quiere que revise el proveedor. Incluya espejos, obras de arte y otras piezas que puedan necesitar instrucciones particulares.

Revise el trabajo y los materiales concretos

Pregunte qué cajas, materiales, mano de obra, desmontaje y desembalaje se incluyen, cuáles son opcionales y quién retira los residuos. Confirme si el embalaje ocurre antes de la recogida o ese mismo día. Si usted embala parte del envío, pida al transportista sus instrucciones y restricciones.

Identifique objetos frágiles y embalajes originales que aún conserva.

Describa los objetos difíciles de levantar o explicar.

Deje documentos, medicamentos y artículos de viaje fuera de la zona de embalaje.

Señale las piezas frágiles antes del embalaje

Anote las medidas de espejos grandes, cuadros enmarcados o tableros de vidrio. Comparta fotos si el coordinador se las pide. Pregunte qué piezas necesitan materiales especiales o cajones a medida y dónde se detalla ese trabajo en el presupuesto.

Explore los servicios de mudanza

Mudanzas de larga distancia

Compare pertenencias, servicios y accesos en cada presupuesto escrito. Tenga a mano su ruta y las fechas que prefiere al solicitar una cotización a Vantage.

Mudanzas residenciales

Haga un inventario por habitación y revise el acceso a ambas viviendas antes de comparar su presupuesto de mudanza.

Mudanzas de oficina

Distinga los muebles, equipos y accesos del plan de reapertura. Pregunte qué puede incluir el transportista antes de comprometer fechas.

Mudanzas de militares y veteranos

Tenga a mano las fechas de presentación y las preguntas de su oficina de transporte. Confirme documentos y capacidad del transportista antes de contratar la mudanza.

Mudanzas para personas mayores

Decida qué cabe, quién puede ayudar con los acuerdos y qué necesita tener a mano. Prepare un inventario claro antes de revisar los servicios.

Servicios de embalaje

Decida si necesita ayuda con una habitación, objetos frágiles o toda la vivienda. Pida que el presupuesto detalle materiales, mano de obra y manipulación.

Preguntas sobre almacenamiento

Pregunte por disponibilidad de almacenamiento para su envío, acceso a los objetos, cargos de manipulación y cómo programar la entrega.

Preguntas sobre transporte de autos

Tenga a mano año, marca, modelo, estado del vehículo y fechas preferidas. Confirme proveedor y condiciones del auto por separado del envío del hogar.

Servicios de embalaje

Preguntas antes de mudarse

¿Quién organiza la mudanza y quién transporta sus pertenencias?

−

Vantage describe su función como intermediario de mudanzas interestatales de bienes del hogar: organiza el transporte con una empresa transportista. El transportista asignado, y no Vantage, realiza el transporte. Identifique esa empresa en los documentos de su envío y guarde sus datos de contacto junto con el presupuesto. Consulte por separado al intermediario y al transportista en el buscador federal. Que una empresa aparezca en un directorio no significa que esté asignada a su mudanza; pida ese dato a su coordinador. Lea las condiciones por escrito antes de contratar servicios.

¿El embalaje cambia la protección de mis pertenencias?

Coordinación de mudanzas

Interestatal

Solicitud de cotización

Gratis

423 reseñas en BBB · Consultado el 12 nov. 2026 · nueva pestaña

↗

Imagen ilustrativa

Prepare un plan de transporte para su auto

Describa el vehículo y la ruta

Indique año, marca y modelo, si el vehículo funciona, sus modificaciones y dónde puede recogerse. Explique cómo se relacionan sus fechas con la mudanza del hogar. Pregunte al coordinador si puede organizarse el transporte del auto para esa ruta y fechas; una consulta no confirma una opción ni un transportista.

Pida las instrucciones de preparación del proveedor

Pregunte qué modalidades están disponibles, qué acceso se necesita para recoger y entregar, y cómo se documentará el estado del vehículo. Confirme directamente con el proveedor las instrucciones sobre llaves, combustible, objetos personales, alarmas y cualquier procedimiento especial.

¿Quién transportará el vehículo y quién coordina las fechas?

¿Cuáles son las condiciones escritas de pago, cancelación y registro del estado del auto?

¿El transporte del vehículo tiene un contrato separado del envío del hogar?

Mantenga coordinados ambos planes

Guarde los contactos del proveedor del vehículo separados de los del envío del hogar. Confirme quién entregará y recibirá el auto, y avise si cambian el acceso o el viaje. Compare por escrito y por separado las ofertas de transporte del vehículo.

Licensed Moving Broker

· USDOT

4078939

· MC

1551322

Vantage Movers is a licensed interstate moving broker. Vantage is not a motor carrier and does not transport household goods. Vantage coordinates and arranges transportation of household goods through FMCSA-authorized motor carriers. Final charges are based on the carrier's tariff, shipment inventory, requested services, route, and move conditions. Carrier tariffs are available for inspection from the carrier upon reasonable request.

Interstate moving · Florida

Your Florida move. A plan for every detail.

From a South Florida condo to a new chapter out of state, start with the details that shape your move. Vantage helps coordinate interstate moving services around your inventory, access and dates.

Explore your move plan

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. Confirm availability and services for your route.

FLORIDA

Let’s plan your Florida move

Moving to or from Florida? Share your actual pickup and destination ZIP codes. We’ll start with your household, then work through the building access and timing.

Interstate coordination

Clear broker & carrier roles

Planning around your inventory

The details that matter

A Florida move deserves more than a generic checklist

Start with the whole household

Include patio furniture, garage shelving and anything in a storage unit. One room-by-room inventory gives the estimate conversation a useful starting point.

Make room for building rules

Ask your condo or HOA about loading areas, elevator reservations and any documents it requires. Share the answers before a carrier is scheduled.

Put dates in writing

Separate your preferred pickup, property handover and delivery availability. Ask which dates are agreed windows and what happens if your keys are delayed.

Know who does what

Vantage coordinates as an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier handles the physical pickup, transportation and delivery.

FLORIDA

Where in Florida is your next chapter?

Use these city starting points to prepare your quote. Share your exact ZIP codes, building details and dates so we can discuss availability for your move.

Miami & Fort Lauderdale

Condo entrance, elevator booking and building contact.

West Palm Beach

Community access, gate instructions and loading arrangements.

Orlando

Lease handover, storage items and delivery contact.

Tampa & St. Petersburg

Parking space, apartment access and pickup inventory.

Jacksonville

Both ZIP codes, driveway access and move-date flexibility.

Fort Myers & Naples

Building rules, outdoor items and arrival arrangements.

Make room for what you need

Build your Florida move around the services you need

Start with the household shipment, then discuss the extras. Each service, provider and charge needs confirmation in your written arrangements.

Arrive prepared

The challenges to plan around in Florida

01

A weather plan that travels with you

Florida’s official hurricane-preparation page places Atlantic hurricane season from June 1 through November 30. Check official notices near your move and ask how a disruption would change pickup or delivery communication.

Keep personal documents, medication and your household emergency supplies accessible during travel. A moving shipment should not be your only access to essentials.

02

Condo access is part of the inventory conversation

Before move day, get your building’s actual instructions for elevators, loading, vehicle access and permitted hours. Rules differ by property; there is no single Florida-wide condo checklist.

Share any reservation or document requirement with your coordinator and the carrier. Ask about access-related charges before approving the written estimate.

Arrive prepared

Get ready for what matters most in Florida

01

Pack for the handover, not just the truck

Choose a first-night bag with clothing, chargers, keys and personal essentials. Keep it with you so arrival does not depend on unpacking a particular box.

Discuss outdoor furniture and garage contents early. Ask the carrier what it cannot accept and how it wants sensitive or unusual items prepared.

02

Arriving in Florida? Make a separate resident checklist

FLHSMV’s new-resident page explains driver license, identification, title and registration processes. Review the current document requirements and timing for your situation before scheduling appointments.

Keep school, utility and property arrangements in the same arrival folder, but confirm each directly with the relevant provider. Your moving quote does not complete those tasks.

Explore a move out of Florida

Heading to North Carolina? Follow a plan that connects your Florida departure with the details at your new address.

Florida to North Carolina

Getting ready for your move to Wisconsin

Plan the delivery address, prepare your paperwork and use official new-resident resources. Start with a practical guide, then confirm arrangements for your own route.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Prepare for the address, not just the state

The useful delivery details are the ones at your actual building: who can let the crew in, where unloading can be discussed, and when the receiving area is available. Ask the property manager about stairs, elevators, loading access and any reservation. Send those answers with the inventory to your coordinator.

This is a Wisconsin preparation guide. Its presence in the directory does not confirm carrier availability, an intrastate service or a Vantage office in Wisconsin. Confirm your origin, destination, requested services and dates in your own quote conversation.

Use Wisconsin's official new-resident instructions

Wisconsin DOT's new-resident page links to driver license or ID information and vehicle title and plate instructions. Use the state page to check which process applies to you, the documents required and the current timing. Keep those tasks separate from the household shipment; this guide does not determine your residency or eligibility.

Gather your existing identification and vehicle records where you can reach them during travel. Check the official instructions before making an appointment or sending documents. The state page also offers Spanish information.

Choose a useful planning path

For an interstate shipment, the long-distance guide helps you prepare the information behind an estimate. The packing guide helps separate what you will pack from work you want a provider to review. Use one room-by-room inventory for both conversations so changes do not get lost.

Long-distance moving: inventory, dates, access and written comparisons.

Packing services: materials, tasks and carrier-specific instructions.

Madison guide: building access, campus-area move-out information and a first-week plan.

Leave room for the day of arrival

Confirm who will receive the shipment if you arrive separately. Keep keys, personal documents, essential medication and the agreement with you. Ask your coordinator and carrier how to communicate a change in the destination or access plan, and save the answer with their contact details.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Wisconsin

Vantage Movers ·

Locations

Getting ready to move to Madison, Wisconsin

Start with the delivery entrance and your building's rules. Then plan for campus-area turnover, state paperwork and a manageable first week in Madison.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Confirm the entrance your delivery will actually use

A street address alone will not tell a carrier which doorway or loading area works for your building. Ask the manager for the delivery entrance, permitted hours, elevator reservation process and a day-of-arrival contact. Measure narrow doors and mention stairs or a long route from parking before the written estimate is finalized.

Keep those answers in a short arrival note. If you are collecting keys at another location, name the person who can meet the shipment. Ask the carrier to review the access plan rather than assuming a curb space or loading zone will be available. This guide concerns Madison in Wisconsin, not another city with the same name.

Near campus in August? Check the current move-out area and rules

Madison's Streets Division publishes a special August student move-out page with an area map, seasonal collection information and instructions for large items and electronics. The special arrangements apply to the defined area and dates, not automatically to every Madison address.

The page checked for this guide described the 2026 season. If your move is in another year, open the city's current notice and confirm the applicable schedule; do not reuse last year's dates. Ask your building whether other residents will be moving at the same time and how it allocates elevators or loading access. City collection guidance is not a reservation for your moving truck.

If you are discarding items before or after the move, use the city instructions or your building's waste provider. Do not leave unwanted electronics or furniture outside based on an assumption that a seasonal collection applies.

Make a first-week folder before the boxes arrive

Keep your lease or home documents, building contacts, shipment paperwork and the assigned carrier's contact information accessible. Add a short list of tasks you can do independently of delivery: collecting keys, confirming utility arrangements and checking the building's recycling instructions.

For driver license, identification or vehicle paperwork, use Wisconsin DOT's new-resident page. It links to the state processes and a Spanish version. Check current document and timing requirements directly with the state rather than relying on a moving checklist to decide what applies to you.

Choose a small first outing, and check access before you go

If you want a simple local destination after unpacking the essentials, Washington Manor Park is listed by Madison Parks with a playground and basketball court. Use its official page to check current information and decide whether it suits your household and location; this guide does not promise that any amenity is available on your arrival date.

For the Lake Monona waterfront, distinguish the existing place from proposed improvements. The city's LakeWay project page described future construction in its October 15, 2026 update, with construction anticipated to begin in June 2027 subject to contract award. The planned improvements are not presented here as open attractions. Check the latest city access and project notices before planning a waterfront outing.

Bring the plan back to your shipment

Return to your inventory with the building answers in hand. Add anything you discovered about stairs, elevator reservations or packing needs, and ask your coordinator whether the written estimate needs updating. Keep a personal arrival bag separate from the shipment so the first evening does not depend on finding a particular box.

Use the Wisconsin directory for state-level preparation or return to the quote with your current details. A city guide is reading material; carrier availability and service arrangements still need confirmation for your own route.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Moving routes

Two states. One considered plan.

Moving from Florida to North Carolina

Close out your Florida home with a clear plan for the other end. From Charlotte and the Triangle to a mountain or coastal address, the details of your North Carolina arrival belong in the conversation from day one.

Explore your move plan

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. Confirm availability and services for your route.

FL → NC

Your Florida → North Carolina move

Tell us your actual Florida pickup and North Carolina destination. Your dates, inventory and access details help shape the next conversation; this guide does not prefill your quote.

Interstate coordination

Clear broker & carrier roles

Planning around your inventory

The details that matter

Plan the departure and the arrival together

Leave Florida with access settled

Confirm your building’s elevator, gate and loading instructions. Include outdoor furniture, garage items and anything in a separate storage unit in the inventory.

Describe the North Carolina address

A Charlotte apartment and a mountain driveway need different access questions. Share stairs, parking, slope, turnarounds and the route from unloading to the door.

Connect keys and delivery

Name the earliest day someone can receive the shipment. If closing or lease dates remain uncertain, ask about storage terms and the process for changing delivery details.

Keep the handover easy to follow

Save broker and carrier contacts with the written estimate and inventory. Confirm who communicates changes and who will meet the carrier at the destination.

us-atlas

FL → NC

Where are you landing in North Carolina?

Start with your new community, then share the exact destination so the carrier can assess the building and access conditions.

Charlotte

For an apartment, ask about loading bays and elevator reservations.

Raleigh & Durham

Coordinate keys, building contacts and the first available receiving date.

Greensboro & Winston-Salem

Check driveway access and space to unload at your specific home.

Asheville & mountain addresses

Ask the carrier to assess grades, narrow access and turnarounds.

Wilmington & coastal addresses

Arrive prepared

Packing, timing and your first days in North Carolina

01

Let the destination forecast shape your arrival bag

Check the forecast for your actual North Carolina town and arrival date. Keep suitable clothing, rain protection and first-night essentials with you, especially if you travel ahead of the shipment.

Ask the carrier how to prepare items that need special handling. A state-to-state guide cannot determine safe packing for your furniture, artwork or equipment.

02

Build a timing plan without guessing a transit time

Keep pickup, your own travel, key collection and the delivery window as separate items on your calendar. Exact cities, carrier arrangements and access conditions matter; the line on this map is not a driving route or a delivery-time estimate.

Use Florida’s official emergency guidance before departure and DriveNC for North Carolina travel information. Recheck near the move; neither this guide nor a saved road notice confirms current conditions.

03

Compare the scope behind the price

Give each estimate conversation the same inventory and access notes. Ask how packing, stairs, a long carry, storage or separate auto transport would be reflected in the written terms.

Read the agreed services, payment arrangements and delivery conditions before signing. Keep updated documents when your inventory or destination details change.

04

Keep North Carolina resident tasks out of the moving boxes

NCDMV’s new-resident resource covers driver licensing, vehicle title and registration, documents and insurance. Use its current instructions to determine what applies to your situation rather than assuming Florida procedures carry over.

Keep identification and vehicle documents accessible. Use USPS’s official forwarding and change-of-address instructions for mail, then add utility activation and your new building contact to a separate arrival checklist.

Keep planning your Florida departure

Return to the Florida guide for building access, household preparation and state resources.

Privacy Policy

June 5, 2026

Your privacy matters to us. This policy explains how we handle your information.

On This Page

How We Use Your Information

Children's Privacy

Changes to This Policy

Contact Us

Vantage Movers ("Vantage Movers," "we," "us," or "our") is a licensed interstate moving broker that arranges household-goods relocations through independent, FMCSA-authorized motor carriers. This Privacy Policy describes how we collect, use, disclose, and protect personal information when you visit our website, request a quote, or otherwise interact with us.

01

Overview

By using our website and submitting a quote request, you acknowledge that you have read and understood this Privacy Policy. If you do not agree with our practices, please do not use the site or provide us with your information. This policy applies to information collected through our website, by phone, by email, and through our online forms.

02

Information We Collect

We collect information that you provide directly to us, information collected automatically, and information from third parties. This may include:

Contact details

- your name, telephone number(s), email address, and physical addresses.

Move details

- origin and destination locations, preferred move dates, inventory size, type of move, and special handling requirements.

Communications

- records of your calls, chats, emails, text messages, and form submissions with us or our partners.

Technical data

- IP address, browser type, device identifiers, pages visited, referring URLs, and similar usage information.

Marketing data

- the source of your inquiry and your interactions with our advertisements or marketing partners.

We do not request or knowingly collect sensitive information such as Social Security numbers, financial account numbers, or government-issued ID numbers through our quote forms.

If you choose the optional pickup-location suggestion and allow your browser to share location, we send approximate coordinates through our server to Google Maps to suggest a pickup ZIP. We do not add those coordinates to your quote, saved form progress or analytics. The suggestion does not change your pickup ZIP until you confirm it. Manual ZIP entry remains available. Google handles the lookup under its

Privacy Policy (opens in a new tab)

.

03

How We Use Your Information

We use the information we collect to:

Prepare and deliver moving estimates and quotes you request.

Arrange and coordinate your relocation with licensed, insured motor carriers.

Respond to inquiries and provide support before, during, and after your move.

Send service updates, scheduling information, and transactional messages.

Send offers, promotions, and marketing communications where permitted by law and consistent with your consent.

Operate, maintain, analyze, and improve our website and services.

Detect and prevent fraud, and comply with legal and regulatory obligations.

04

Communications & Consent

When you submit a quote request, you authorize Vantage Movers and the moving service providers we partner with to contact you at the telephone number(s) and email address you provided - including by phone call, text/SMS message, and email - regarding your move and related offers.

You agree that these communications may be made using automated technology, and that consent is not a condition of any purchase. Message and data rates may apply. You may opt out of marketing calls and texts at any time by replying

STOP

to a text message, by following unsubscribe instructions in emails, or by contacting us directly.

05

How We Share Information

As a moving broker, sharing your information is essential to arranging your relocation. We may share your information with:

Motor carriers and moving partners

- independently owned, FMCSA-authorized carriers and affiliated providers who quote, schedule, and perform your move.

Service providers

- vendors who support hosting, analytics, communications, and customer-support operations.

Marketing partners

- where you have engaged with a partner advertisement or where permitted by law.

Legal and safety

- authorities or third parties when required by law, to enforce agreements, or to protect rights, property, or safety.

Business transfers

- in connection with a merger, acquisition, financing, or sale of assets.

The carriers and partners that receive your information are separate business entities responsible for their own privacy practices. We encourage you to review their policies directly.

06

Cookies & Tracking Technologies

We and our partners use cookies, web beacons, pixels, and similar technologies to operate the site, remember preferences, measure performance, and deliver relevant advertising. You can control cookies through browser settings, though disabling them may affect site functionality.

07

Data Retention

We retain personal information for as long as necessary to fulfill the purposes described in this policy, including providing services, complying with legal obligations, resolving disputes, and enforcing agreements. When information is no longer needed, we take reasonable steps to delete or de-identify it.

08

Security

We use reasonable administrative, technical, and physical safeguards designed to protect your personal information. However, no method of transmission over the Internet or electronic storage is completely secure, and we cannot guarantee absolute security.

09

Your Privacy Rights

Depending on where you live, you may have rights regarding your personal information, including the right to request access to, correction of, or deletion of your information, and the right to opt out of certain uses. To exercise these rights, contact us using the details below.

We will not discriminate against you for exercising any of your privacy rights, such as by denying services or providing a different level or quality of service.

10

Do Not Sell or Share My Personal Information

We do not sell your personal information for money. Certain sharing with marketing or carrier partners may be considered a "sale" or "sharing" for targeted advertising under some state laws. You may opt out by emailing

support@vantagehomemovers.com

with the subject line "Do Not Sell My Personal Information," or by calling

(888) 477-9232

.

11

Third-Party Links

Our website may contain links to third-party websites and services that we do not operate or control. This Privacy Policy does not apply to those sites, and we encourage you to review their policies before providing information.

12

Children's Privacy

Our services are intended for adults and are not directed to children under 18. We do not knowingly collect personal information from children.

13

Changes to This Policy

We may update this Privacy Policy from time to time. When we do, we will revise the "Last updated" date at the top of this page. Your continued use of the website after changes are posted constitutes acceptance of the updated policy.

14

Contact Us

If you have questions about this Privacy Policy or wish to exercise your privacy rights, please reach out to us.

Questions to help plan your move

General planning guidance. For shipment dates, services or terms, check your paperwork and speak with our team.

Who transports my belongings?

A motor carrier transports the shipment. A moving broker arranges transportation. Check the carrier name and contact details on your shipment paperwork; requesting a quote does not identify the carrier for your move.

FMCSA: movers and brokers

(

new tab

)

How should I compare two estimates?

Start with the same belongings and services. Mark differences in packing, access, storage and delivery terms, then ask for those differences to be addressed in writing before choosing an estimate.

FMCSA moving checklist

(

new tab

)

What should I prepare for the inventory call?

Walk through every room, closet and storage area. Note items that need special handling and questions about access at both buildings. Keep a list of additions or removals to discuss with your coordinator.

Before the inventory call: a room-by-room checklist

Walk through every room and storage space. Separate what is moving, what needs packing help and what will stay with you.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Illustrative image

In this guide

Build one list you can update

Living areas and bedrooms

Kitchen, utility areas and overlooked storage

Record access at both ends

Keep a change log for your coordinator

Build one list you can update

Use a separate line for each large item and a reasonable box estimate for smaller belongings. Mark 'moving', 'not moving' or 'undecided'. Keep the undecided items visible and ask how to update the estimate when you make a decision. This preparation list is not the carrier's final shipment inventory.

Living areas and bedrooms

Open doors and look behind furniture. Note sofas, tables, chairs, beds, dressers, shelving, rugs, lamps and mirrors. Record pieces that need disassembly, glass protection or help passing through a doorway. Include closets and under-bed storage instead of counting only what is visible.

Living / dining room: large pieces, fragile displays, rugs and lamps.

Each bedroom: bed frame, mattress, drawers, closet contents and mirrors.

Home office: desk, chairs, shelves and equipment; keep sensitive records with you.

Kitchen, utility areas and overlooked storage

Count the cupboards you will pack and note appliances that belong to you. Review connection or disconnection needs separately. Then check laundry spaces, attic, basement, garage, shed, balcony and any rented storage unit. Ask the carrier what it will accept before packing chemicals, fuel, batteries or other restricted items.

Kitchen: dishes, glassware, small appliances and pantry decisions.

Garage / shed: tools, garden equipment and large or awkward pieces.

Outdoor spaces: patio set, planters, grill and sports equipment; confirm preparation rules.

Off-site storage: address, access hours, inventory and whether pickup there is part of the plan.

Record access at both ends

For pickup and delivery, note the floor, stair flights, elevator booking, loading entrance, parking contact and building hours. Measure unusually large furniture and narrow openings. Ask who is responsible for arranging access and what to do if the truck cannot reach the expected place.

Pickup notes: ______________________________

Delivery notes: ____________________________

Building contact / reservation: ________________

Keep a change log for your coordinator

Write additions, removals and new service requests with the date. Send the changes before pickup and ask whether the written estimate must be revised. Keep personal documents, medicines, keys and immediate travel needs separate from the shipment.

Added / removed item and quantity: ______________

Packing or handling question: _________________

Date sent / answer received: __________________

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Before the inventory call

Print worksheet

Review the finished list with your coordinator and keep the carrier's written inventory and shipment documents separately.

Your inventory

Area or question

Items / quantity

Packing, access or changes

Living and dining areas

Bedroom 1 / closet

Other bedrooms / closets

Kitchen / cupboards

Office / shelves

Laundry / utility / basement / attic

Garage / shed

Patio / balcony / outdoor items

Off-site storage

Fragile or oversized pieces

Pickup access

Delivery access

Items staying with me

Changes to send to coordinator

Planning guide

4

Comparing two written moving estimates

Put the inventories side by side first. Use this printable worksheet to find differences in services, access, storage and terms before choosing a mover.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Illustrative image

In this guide

Make the two offers describe the same move

How to use the blank worksheet

Worked example: a lower initial number covers less work

The decision still needs written answers

Make the two offers describe the same move

If one estimate includes the patio set, packing the kitchen and a flight of stairs while the other leaves them out, their totals answer different questions. Mark each difference, then ask for the agreed inventory, services and terms in writing. FMCSA's moving checklist directs consumers to compare both costs and services and to check the companies' registration.

Write each company's legal name, contact information and USDOT number at the top of your own copy. Record the estimate date and revision. Ask which company will transport the shipment, then check that company separately from any broker.

How to use the blank worksheet

For every row, write the actual wording or a document reference. Write 'not stated' when an item is missing; a blank is not an inclusion. Use the open-questions row to record who will answer and when. Ask for a revised written estimate if the scope changes, and keep the old version with your notes.

Use the same pickup and delivery addresses, dates and inventory for both requests.

Separate what is included, optional, excluded and still unconfirmed.

Compare estimate type, payment schedule, cancellation terms and shipment protection as well as the total.

Read the applicable documents before signing; this worksheet does not replace them.

Worked example: a lower initial number covers less work

Fictional exercise only. Alex and Sam are moving a two-bedroom household with 40 boxes, a patio set, kitchen packing, one flight of stairs and a seven-day storage gap. Offer A is invented at $4,800 and includes that scope. Offer B is invented at $4,100 but leaves four items out. Every dollar below is made up to demonstrate arithmetic; these are not Vantage prices, market rates or a quote.

In the exercise, B's writer supplies hypothetical additions of $175 for the patio set, $450 for kitchen packing, $125 for stairs and $100 for the seven-day storage gap. The additions total $850. On those fictional assumptions, B becomes $4,950, which is $150 above A. In a real comparison, only the provider's revised written estimate can supply these amounts. Do not fill unknown charges with this example's numbers.

The decision still needs written answers

Even after matching scope, a total does not settle everything. In the example, Alex and Sam still need to confirm the assigned carrier, estimate terms, delivery arrangements and what happens if storage lasts longer. They request those answers and updated documents before choosing. You can use the same process without submitting this worksheet or your personal move details to Vantage.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Worked comparison

Print worksheet

Fictional offer A

Fictional offer B

Kitchen packing excluded initially. Fictional addition: +$450.

Stairs / long carries

One flight included; no long carry assumed at either address.

Stairs omitted initially. Fictional addition: +$125. Same no-long-carry assumption needs confirmation.

Storage

Storage excluded initially. Fictional addition for seven days: +$100.

Redelivery

One delivery after the seven-day gap included in this exercise.

One delivery after the seven-day gap included in this exercise. Extra attempts remain a question.

Original invented total

$4,800

$4,100

$0

$175 + $450 + $125 + $100 = $850

$4,800

$4,100 + $850 = $4,950

Assigned carrier; estimate type; payment/cancellation terms; extended storage; protection terms.

Same questions, plus a revised written estimate showing all four additions.

In this fictional example the $700 lower initial figure becomes $150 higher after matching scope. Get the real provider's written answers; neither amount predicts your move's cost.

Planning guide

2

min read

What should stay out of a moving box until you ask?

Make a separate review list for potentially hazardous, perishable, personal and unusual belongings. Get the assigned carrier's restrictions before packing.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Illustrative image

In this guide

Get the carrier's actual restricted-items list

Build an ask-before-packing list

Separate essentials from transport restrictions

Resolve each item before sealing the box

Get the carrier's actual restricted-items list

A general checklist cannot authorize an item for transport. Ask the assigned carrier for its current instructions and identify anything you cannot confidently describe. FMCSA warns that offering hazardous materials for transportation brings specific shipper requirements. Do not hide a questionable item inside an ordinary box.

Build an ask-before-packing list

Walk through the garage, utility cupboard, kitchen and garden storage separately from the furniture inventory. Write down the product name and the concern; keep labels available for the carrier's review. These are examples to discuss, not a complete list of prohibited goods or permission to ship.

Fuel, paint, solvents, cleaners and compressed containers.

Batteries and powered equipment with special preparation instructions.

Food, plants and other items that may not tolerate time in transit.

Unusual or regulated belongings that need an individual answer.

Separate essentials from transport restrictions

Something you choose to carry yourself is not necessarily prohibited. Create a personal travel bag for documents, keys, medication, chargers and everyday essentials. Plan separately for pets and family travel. Do not put private documents or medical details into an online moving inquiry.

Resolve each item before sealing the box

Record the carrier's answer beside each item: accepted with instructions, excluded, or still awaiting review. Keep unresolved items outside the shipment. If something cannot travel, ask the appropriate local authority about safe handling or disposal; do not improvise by draining, mixing or discarding materials.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Planning guide

What can change the cost of your move?

Make a scope list before setting a budget: belongings, packing, access, storage and delivery arrangements all need written answers.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Illustrative image

In this guide

Start with the work, not a price per bedroom

List the details that deserve a line item

Ask which estimate terms apply

Keep a revision trail

Start with the work, not a price per bedroom

Two homes with the same bedroom count can contain very different shipments. Sketch your own cost worksheet with four columns: task, included or optional, written amount, and unanswered question. Leave unknown amounts blank until the provider supplies them. This guide contains no market rates or Vantage price promises.

List the details that deserve a line item

Use one inventory for every conversation. Note who packs each room, whether furniture needs attention, the route into each building and any gap between leaving and arriving. Ask which details affect the written offer for your shipment.

Packing: labor, boxes, wrapping and any unpacking requested.

Access: stairs, elevator bookings, distance from parking and restricted loading hours.

Storage: the planned period, handling, access and the process for final delivery.

Changes: how additions, revised addresses or changed arrangements will be documented.

Ask which estimate terms apply

FMCSA advises getting a written estimate covering the charges and explains that a non-binding estimate does not guarantee final cost. Ask the provider to identify the estimate type and explain it using your actual inventory. A website estimate or initial conversation does not replace the shipment documents.

Keep a revision trail

Put the date and version on your worksheet. If you add a sofa or change the delivery entrance, note when you told the coordinator and where the updated terms appear. Keep payment timing, cancellation questions and unresolved items together. Compare the final written scope with your plan before agreeing; do not substitute an example from another household.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Keep your moving paperwork useful from quote to delivery.

Build one folder for estimates, inventory revisions, contact details and open questions, with a copy you can reach while travelling.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Illustrative image

In this guide

Make a simple document index

Read the document in front of you

Turn uncertainty into a written question

Keep the folder available after pickup

Make a simple document index

Start with four dividers: proposal, agreed arrangements, pickup, and delivery. Add a cover sheet listing each company's role and contact, your document dates and anything still awaiting an answer. Keep sensitive details in your own folder; the worksheet does not need to be uploaded to this website.

Read the document in front of you

FMCSA identifies the estimate, bill of lading and inventory among important shipment documents. Ask the provider to explain each document's purpose, the charges and services, and any blanks or differences from the earlier version. Keep a copy of what you sign and the applicable consumer information.

Turn uncertainty into a written question

Use a short question log with a date, contact and answer location. For example: which company is transporting the shipment; how are pickup and delivery communicated; what payment and cancellation terms apply; and where are valuation and claim instructions? Ask for the answer in the relevant paperwork, not just in a note beside an estimate.

Keep the folder available after pickup

Carry the contacts and documents where you can reach them during travel. Add inventory observations and delivery paperwork without discarding earlier versions. If a concern arises, use the provider's documented contact and claim process; Vantage's contact page also offers the existing support channel. This organizing guide does not create a new complaint service, a response deadline or a refund entitlement.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Who handles my move after I request a quote?

Keep three roles clear: you describe the shipment, Vantage coordinates the request, and the assigned motor carrier transports the goods.

Request a moving quote

Explore moving guides

Illustrative image

In this guide

Your part: explain the shipment and review the agreement

Vantage's role: arranging transportation

The carrier's role: pickup, transportation and delivery

Build a contact card before pickup

Check the official source yourself

Your part: explain the shipment and review the agreement

Give an up-to-date inventory, route, preferred dates and access information. Ask questions about anything missing or unclear in the estimate. Keep the documents and tell the relevant contact when the inventory, address or plan changes. A submitted quote request is a request for follow-up, not a completed booking.

Vantage's role: arranging transportation

Vantage's existing business disclosure identifies it as an interstate household goods moving broker. Its coordinator is the contact for discussing your request and the proposed arrangements. Vantage does not transport your household goods; a motor carrier does. Ask for that carrier's identity and contact information and keep it with the written estimate.

A broker and a carrier are different businesses with different roles. FMCSA explains the distinction and provides a lookup to check registration. Do not treat a list of companies as confirmation that a particular one has been assigned to your shipment.

The carrier's role: pickup, transportation and delivery

Confirm pickup and delivery arrangements with the contacts named in your shipment paperwork. Review the inventory and other completed documents, and keep a copy. At delivery, follow the carrier's documented process for recording missing or damaged items and raising concerns. The federal moving checklist explains preparation, moving-day and delivery-day responsibilities.

Build a contact card before pickup

Write the coordinator, assigned carrier, building contacts and your own delivery contact on one page. Include the shipment reference and where the agreement is saved. For scheduling or shipment concerns, start with the responsible contact shown in the documents. For questions about Vantage's coordination, use the existing Vantage contact destination. Do not assume an unverified department or response-time promise.

Vantage coordinator / contact: __________________

Assigned carrier / dispatch contact: ______________

Pickup and delivery building contacts: ____________

Reference / document location: _________________

Written complaint or claim instructions: ___________

Check the official source yourself

Use FMCSA's mover lookup to review the company name, business type and registration information. Read the official consumer materials and the terms for your own shipment. The source links below open the authorities directly; this article is a planning aid, not a substitute for your agreement.

Vantage is an interstate moving broker. The assigned motor carrier performs pickup, transport and delivery. A quote request does not reserve a carrier or a date.

Prepare a separate plan for your car

Describe the vehicle and the route

Start with the year, make and model, whether the vehicle operates, any modifications and where it can be collected. Explain how its timing relates to your household move. Ask your coordinator whether vehicle shipping can be arranged for the route and dates; an inquiry does not confirm an option or a carrier.

Ask for the provider's preparation instructions

Ask which transport methods are available, what pickup and delivery access is required, and how vehicle condition will be documented. Confirm instructions for keys, fuel, personal belongings and any alarm or special operating procedure directly with the provider.

Who will transport the vehicle and who handles scheduling?

What are the written payment, cancellation and condition-reporting terms?

Is vehicle transport a separate agreement from the household shipment?

Keep the two plans coordinated

Save the vehicle provider's contacts separately from those for the household shipment. Confirm who can hand over and receive the car, and tell the provider if the access or travel plan changes. Use a separate written comparison if you receive more than one vehicle-shipping offer.

Questions before you move

Who arranges the move, and who transports it?

−

Vantage describes its role as an interstate household goods moving broker: arranging transportation with a motor carrier. The assigned carrier, rather than Vantage, performs the transportation. Identify that company on your shipment paperwork and keep its contact details with your estimate. Use the federal mover lookup to check the broker and carrier separately. A directory listing does not identify the company assigned to your particular shipment; ask your coordinator for that information. Read the written terms before agreeing to services.

Are open and enclosed transport always available?

+

What if my car cannot be driven to a loading area?

+

Plan the office move around your reopening

Decide what must be ready first

List workstations, shared furniture, equipment and boxed files by department. Mark the pieces that need to be placed first so your own team can resume work. Keep your business continuity plan separate from transport dates; ask which pickup and delivery arrangements can actually be confirmed.

Prepare a handoff between both buildings

Choose one authorized contact for each location. Ask about loading docks, lift dimensions, delivery hours, security procedures and building documentation. Keep equipment shutdown, data protection and network setup with the appropriate people in your organization unless a separate written agreement assigns that work.

Label furniture and boxes by destination room or workstation.

Identify leased, sensitive and unusually heavy equipment for individual review.

Record building contacts and who approves changes to the moving scope.

Keep changes visible

If the floor plan or item list changes, send a revised version before pickup. Ask the coordinator to identify any effect on services or timing in writing. Keep staff relocation needs separate from the office inventory so each request gets the appropriate review.

Office moving

Questions before you move

Who arranges the move, and who transports it?

−

Vantage describes its role as an interstate household goods moving broker: arranging transportation with a motor carrier. The assigned carrier, rather than Vantage, performs the transportation. Identify that company on your shipment paperwork and keep its contact details with your estimate. Use the federal mover lookup to check the broker and carrier separately. A directory listing does not identify the company assigned to your particular shipment; ask your coordinator for that information. Read the written terms before agreeing to services.

Pricing context

How much does a long-distance move cost?

Distance is only one part of the estimate. Your inventory, access, dates and requested services also matter. Compare estimates using the same inventory and ask for a written estimate for your route.

Illustrative ranges published by Safe Ship. Ask Vantage for a written estimate for your route and discuss pickup and delivery windows.

Route

Planning range

Transit after pickup

Regional interstate · 500–1,000 miles

$2,000–$5,500

2–5 days

Long-haul interstate · 1,000–2,000 miles

$3,500–$8,000

5–10 days

Cross-country / coast-to-coast

$4,500–$10,000+

7–14 days

Reference checked November 12, 2026:

Safe Ship ·

long-distance planning context

.

The source does not supply an inventory or sample that validates these examples for Vantage. Review your own written estimate and dates.

What can raise the price

High-demand dates and a narrow pickup window.

Packing, specialty items, stairs or a long carry from the truck.

Storage and extra handling between pickup and delivery.

How to keep the comparison useful

Ask whether alternative dates change the offer for your route.

Confirm your inventory before comparing totals.

Agree what you will pack and what each written offer includes.

Common options to discuss; these are not packages or guaranteed prices.

Option

What to confirm

Cost considerations

Transport with self-packing

You prepare the boxes; confirm the inventory and furniture protection.

Materials, stairs, carrying distances and additional services.

Transport with packing help

Ask which items, materials and labor are included in writing.

Partial or full packing, fragile items and unpacking.

Moving with storage

Explain any gap between pickup and delivery.

Duration, handling, access and later delivery charges.

Confirm the estimate type, excluded services and how inventory changes are handled. A quote request does not reserve a date or carrier.

Request your moving quote →

Read the moving cost guide →

Plan both ends of your interstate move

Start with the same inventory

A two-bedroom home is a starting point, not an item list. Walk through every room, open closets and include the garage, balcony and storage unit. Mark furniture you will sell separately from the items you intend to ship. If that list changes after the conversation, ask for the estimate to be updated.

What to prepare for your coordinator

Bring a preferred pickup period and the earliest date someone can receive the shipment. Explain where a truck can stop at both addresses. Ask which dates and services can be confirmed for your route before making arrangements that depend on them.

Inventory, approximate box count and unusual or fragile items.

Stairs, elevator reservations, loading entrance and distance from parking to the door.

Packing help, a possible storage gap and a contact available at delivery.

Compare the work before comparing the total

Use the estimates worksheet to put two written offers side by side. If one omits packing or assumes an elevator that cannot be reserved, the totals describe different work. Ask each company to explain the differences and identify the applicable estimate terms in writing.

Illustrative image

Build your plan around orders and housing dates

Separate official requirements from the moving quote

Write down the dates and records your transportation or finance office tells you to use. Ask that office to confirm the rules that apply to your own move. A commercial quote conversation cannot establish eligibility, reimbursement or government authorization.

Prepare your questions before reserving services

Describe the household inventory, housing access and any gap between leaving one home and entering the next. If your move requires weight tickets or other records, ask the coordinator and proposed carrier whether they can supply exactly what your office requests before you agree to the arrangement.

Bring the required document names and instructions, without uploading sensitive orders to a public form.

Identify dates that are fixed and dates that can change.

Keep essential records and items your household needs during travel with you.

Keep records and travel essentials together

Keep a folder with the service agreement, inventory, carrier contact and any weight tickets you receive. Carry it with you on the trip so you can answer questions even if the shipment is still in transit. Ask your transportation office where to submit the records it requests.

With gratitude, all year long

Honoring your service. And the family beside you.

To our service members, veterans and military families: thank you. Every new assignment is also a change for a household, and deserves time, attention and thoughtful planning.

A new destination. A clear plan.

Prepare for your family’s next chapter.

Bring your dates, inventory and questions. For a military move, check authorizations and requirements with your transportation office before booking services.

Talk through your move →

Veterans Day is observed on November 11. Our appreciation continues throughout the year.

Illustrative image

Decide what to pack and where you need help

Separate the packing list from the shipping list

A piece may be travelling without needing packing help, while a small cupboard of dishes may need substantial preparation. Mark each room with what you will pack and what you want the provider to review. Include mirrors, artwork and other pieces that may need individual handling instructions.

Discuss the actual work and materials

Ask which boxes, wrapping materials, labor, disassembly and unpacking are included, which are optional and who will remove packing debris. Confirm whether packing happens before or on pickup day. If you are packing some items yourself, ask the carrier for its instructions and restrictions.

Identify fragile items and any original packaging you still have.

Tell the coordinator about objects that are difficult to lift or describe.

Keep documents, medications and travel essentials out of the packing area.

Flag fragile pieces before packing day

Note the dimensions of large mirrors, framed art or glass tabletops. Share photos if the coordinator requests them. Ask which pieces need special materials or crating and where that work appears in the estimate.

Packing services

Questions before you move

Who arranges the move, and who transports it?

−

Vantage describes its role as an interstate household goods moving broker: arranging transportation with a motor carrier. The assigned carrier, rather than Vantage, performs the transportation. Identify that company on your shipment paperwork and keep its contact details with your estimate. Use the federal mover lookup to check the broker and carrier separately. A directory listing does not identify the company assigned to your particular shipment; ask your coordinator for that information. Read the written terms before agreeing to services.

Does packing change protection for my belongings?

+

What should I do if I add a room?

+

Can I pack some rooms and request help with others?

+

Illustrative image

Get both homes ready for moving day

Walk the whole home, including the easy-to-miss spaces

Start at the entry and work room by room. Include the furniture behind a closed door, shelves in the garage and items stored elsewhere. Keep a separate list for things you will carry yourself, donate or leave behind. A short video or measurements can help you describe bulky pieces, but ask what your coordinator needs for the inventory review.

Describe the path from the truck to each room

An apartment move starts before the elevator door. Ask the building manager about permitted delivery hours, elevator reservations, loading access and any documents the carrier must provide. At a house, note narrow driveways, steps, low branches and a safe place to discuss unloading with the carrier.

Record the floor and number of stair flights at each address.

Measure oversized pieces and the doors they must pass through.

Identify who can authorize access if you are travelling.

Keep the first night separate

Choose a small set of items you will need before the shipment is unpacked: personal documents, medication, chargers and a change of clothes. Tell the carrier which belongings are staying with you, and review its restrictions before packing unusual items.

423 customer reviews on BBB · Checked Nov 12, 2026 · opens a new tab

Illustrative image

Plan what will fit, who will help and what stays close

Make decisions one room at a time

Use the new home's measurements to decide what to take. Start with everyday furniture and personal keepsakes, then work through cupboards and storage. Keep decisions about selling, donating and shipping in separate lists so an item does not accidentally enter the moving inventory twice.

Choose who can help coordinate

Decide who will join the estimate conversation and who can answer questions at each address. Ask how to authorize a family member or other trusted person to discuss the arrangements. If the destination is a managed community, ask its staff about arrival hours, elevator reservations and receiving procedures.

Keep medication, personal records and daily essentials accessible.

Identify fragile, unusually heavy or sentimental pieces for individual discussion.

Ask which packing or handling tasks can be included in writing.

Set up the first day in your new home

Pack a bag with medication, chargers, glasses and a change of clothes. Share a simple floor plan with the person meeting the shipment and mark where the bed, chairs and other everyday furniture should go. Confirm any delivery instructions with the carrier.

Illustrative image

Plan the gap between pickup and delivery

Explain the gap, including what you do not know

Give your earliest move-out date and the earliest date the destination could receive the shipment. Say whether that date is confirmed or depends on a closing, lease or construction work. Ask the coordinator what arrangements can be considered for your route; this page does not confirm a storage facility or space reservation.

Ask what happens between pickup and delivery

Before agreeing to storage, ask who holds the goods, how long the stated terms apply and how to request delivery. Ask whether you can access items during storage and what notice, handling or redelivery charges would apply. Describe anything with temperature, humidity or special handling requirements and ask for an explicit answer.

Who is the storage provider, and whom do I contact?

What charges apply if the gap lasts longer than expected?

What written protection and claim terms apply while the goods are stored?

Compare the whole journey

Use the estimate worksheet to compare pickup, storage, handling and final delivery together. Ask what would change if the delivery address or date changes. Save the written instructions for requesting redelivery with the provider's contact details.

Destination Planning

Start With Your Route

Choose a state to start planning, or browse the destination guides. Guide availability is separate from service availability. Ask your coordinator to confirm your route, dates and requested services.

Florida

Planning an interstate move involving Florida? Have both addresses, your inventory and preferred dates ready. Ask your coordinator to confirm carrier availability and services for your specific route. Selecting a state here does not confirm service coverage or set your quote destination.

Discuss Your Route

Explore our Wisconsin and Madison guides. Guide availability is separate from service coverage; confirm your route with Vantage.

Browse destination guides →

Explore moving services

Long-distance moving

Compare the belongings, services and access in each written estimate. Bring your route and preferred dates to a Vantage quote conversation.

Residential moving

Build a room-by-room inventory and a practical access plan for both homes before reviewing your moving estimate.

Office moving

Separate furniture, equipment and access needs from your reopening plan. Ask which work a carrier can include before committing to dates.

Military and veteran moves

Bring your reporting dates and questions from your transportation office. Confirm documents and carrier capabilities before arranging an interstate move.

Senior moving

Decide what will fit, who can help with arrangements and what you need close by. Build a clear inventory before reviewing moving services.

Packing services

Decide whether you need help with a room, fragile pieces or the whole home. Ask for materials, labor and handling to be described in the estimate.

Storage questions

Ask about storage availability for your shipment, access while stored, handling charges and the process for scheduling delivery.

Vehicle transport questions

Have the vehicle's year, make, model, condition and preferred dates ready. Confirm the provider and terms for vehicle shipping separately from household goods.

Storage questions

Questions before you move

Who arranges the move, and who transports it?

−

Vantage describes its role as an interstate household goods moving broker: arranging transportation with a motor carrier. The assigned carrier, rather than Vantage, performs the transportation. Identify that company on your shipment paperwork and keep its contact details with your estimate. Use the federal mover lookup to check the broker and carrier separately. A directory listing does not identify the company assigned to your particular shipment; ask your coordinator for that information. Read the written terms before agreeing to services.

Does storage mean climate-controlled access whenever I need it?

+

What if I do not know when my new home will be ready?

+

SMS Privacy Policy

Last updated:

June 2026

This policy applies specifically to SMS messages from our moving quote program.

On This Page

Overview

Information We Collect

How We Use Your Information

SMS Communications

Information Sharing

Data Security

Your Rights

Contact Information

Vantage Movers sends SMS messages only to customers who opt in on our website quote form. This SMS Privacy Policy explains how we use mobile numbers and SMS consent information for quote requests, scheduling updates, appointment reminders, service updates, and customer support.

01

Overview

This SMS Privacy Policy applies to the Vantage Movers SMS Program. It supplements our general

Privacy Policy

and describes privacy practices that apply to text messaging consent and communications.

02

Information We Collect

Your mobile phone number.

Your SMS opt-in status and the date, time, page, or form where consent was submitted.

Quote request details you provide, such as your name, email, pickup ZIP, destination ZIP, move date, and move size.

SMS communication records, including delivery status, replies, opt-out requests, and support requests.

03

How We Use Your Information

To respond to your moving quote request.

To send appointment scheduling messages, appointment reminders, service updates, and customer support messages.

To manage your SMS opt-in, opt-out, and help requests.

To maintain compliance records for carrier and messaging-program requirements.

To protect our services, prevent abuse, and comply with legal obligations.

04

SMS Communications

If you opt in, Vantage Movers may send SMS messages about your moving quote request, scheduling updates, appointment reminders, service updates, and customer support. Message frequency may vary based on your move request and service needs, up to 4 messages per month. Message and data rates may apply.

You can reply

STOP

to unsubscribe from SMS messages at any time. You can reply

HELP

for assistance or contact us at

(888) 477-9232

or

support@vantagehomemovers.com

. SMS consent is optional and is not required to submit a quote request.

05

Information Sharing

We do not sell or share mobile information or SMS consent data with third parties for their marketing or promotional purposes. SMS consent is not shared with third parties or affiliates for marketing purposes.

We may share information with service providers that help us operate our website, process quote requests, deliver SMS messages, provide customer support, maintain security, or comply with legal obligations. These providers are permitted to use the information only to provide services to us.

06

Data Security

We use reasonable safeguards designed to protect SMS consent records and mobile information. No method of transmission or storage is completely secure, but we work to protect your information from unauthorized access, use, or disclosure.

07

Your Rights

You may opt out of SMS messages at any time by replying

STOP

.

You may request assistance by replying

HELP

or contacting us directly.

You may ask us to update, correct, or delete your contact information, subject to legal and operational requirements.

You may contact us with questions about how your SMS consent information is used.

08

Contact Information

For questions about this SMS Privacy Policy or our SMS program, contact Vantage Movers at

(888) 477-9232

,

support@vantagehomemovers.com

, or 1880 N Congress Ave Ste 401A, Boynton Beach, FL 33426.

Terms & Conditions

Last updated:

June 2026

General website terms for quote requests and broker services.

On This Page

Acceptance of Terms

Services Description

User Submissions & Accuracy

Communications

No Guarantee of Estimate / Broker Disclosure

Limitation of Liability

Governing Law

Contact Information

These Terms & Conditions govern your use of the Vantage Movers website and quote request services. By using our website or submitting a quote request, you agree to these terms.

01

Acceptance of Terms

By accessing this website, requesting a quote, or communicating with us through the site, you agree to be bound by these Terms & Conditions and all applicable laws. If you do not agree, do not use the website or submit information through our forms.

02

Services Description

Vantage Movers is a moving broker. We help consumers request moving quotes and coordinate household-goods relocations through independent, FMCSA-authorized motor carriers. We do not physically transport household goods.

03

User Submissions & Accuracy

You agree to provide accurate, complete, and current information when submitting a quote request. Estimates, scheduling, and carrier availability may depend on the accuracy of the information you provide, including origin and destination ZIP codes, move date, inventory size, and contact information. Optional location suggestions use Google Maps and are subject to the

Google Maps Additional Terms of Service (opens in a new tab)

. Confirm any suggested ZIP before using it.

04

Communications

By submitting a quote request, you ask Vantage Movers to contact you about your request by phone, email, or other contact details you provide. SMS opt-in is optional and is not required to submit a quote request. If you choose to receive SMS messages, those messages are governed by our

SMS Terms & Conditions

.

05

No Guarantee of Estimate / Broker Disclosure

Vantage Movers is a licensed interstate moving broker. Vantage is not a motor carrier and does not transport household goods. Vantage coordinates and arranges transportation of household goods through FMCSA-authorized motor carriers. Final charges are based on the carrier's tariff, shipment inventory, requested services, route, and move conditions. Carrier tariffs are available for inspection from the carrier upon reasonable request.

Any estimate provided through the website is for informational purposes only and is not a guaranteed final price. Final charges may vary based on carrier tariffs, actual services requested, inventory, access conditions, packing needs, storage, timing, and other move-specific factors.

06

Limitation of Liability

Vantage Movers will not be liable for indirect, incidental, consequential, special, punitive, or exemplary damages arising from your use of the website, quote tools, or communications with us. Our liability for any claim related to the website or quote request process is limited to the amount you paid directly to us, if any, for the specific service giving rise to the claim.

07

Governing Law

These Terms & Conditions are governed by the laws of the State of Florida, without regard to conflict-of-law rules.

08

Contact Information

Questions about these Terms & Conditions may be directed to Vantage Movers at

(888) 477-9232

,

support@vantagehomemovers.com

, or 1880 N Congress Ave Ste 401A, Boynton Beach, FL 33426.
